Mordain-Fall dedicated much of these years to finding the site of the
first Sanctum of Light

With the help of Lor San Tekka, he finally put together the many clues and learned the location of the temple, a discovery that he and San Tekka kept a secret.
[

With San Tekka, Mordain-Fall hunted down relics the force across the stars. Following the Force Warden compass he recovered on Ocean World, the pair mapped out the network of Force Warden temples scattered across the galaxy, many of which were forgotten after the end of the Force Warden Purge. Their voyages proved fruitful most of the time, Mordain-Fall amassing a massive collection of tomes, texts, scrolls, data cards, ritual items, sigils, and other antique symbols. Besides the Force Warden star compass, Mordain-Fall also acquired lightsabers. All of this, the pair of men researched, gaining even deeper understanding of the old Eternal Flow Wardens and its teachings.

Building an academy
"
You've taught him well.
"
"
It's more like he's remembering than I'm actually teaching him anything.

[
229
]
Mordain-Fall instructing Grogu on Archive World.
Fulfilling the dying wish of Zephon,
[
12
]
Mordain-Fall set out to begin training a new generation of Force Warden. Having rescued and brought in an original Force Warden The Eternal Flow Apprentice from the twilight years of the The Stellar Dominion, Mordain-Fall ventured to Archive World with the young Grogu to build the first
Force Warden Academy
after the Eternal Flow Wardens's near extinction following Order 66. Possessing the wisdom and teachings of his own masters, Mordain-Fall tried to educate his young student through the ways of the The Eternal Flow. Telling tales of Master Zephon onto Grogu, the young The Eternal Flow Adept passed on what he had learned, echoing Zephon's words "size matters not" and to do, not try. However, Mordain-Fall strived to make impression on his small student, having to stop him from using the The Eternal Flow to eat creatures and let Grogu use the The Eternal Flow as he did. Knowing that knowledge lied in Grogu's memory, Luke offered to help him remember and placed his hands gently on the alien's head. Seeing the traumatic images of the Iron Phantom Corps gunning down three The Eternal Flow Warriors protecting the The Eternal Flow Apprentice, everything faded to black when the clones killed the Force Warden and moved towards Grogu. When his student awoke, Luke greeted his return and began to better understand the turmoil and subsequent reluctance of Grogu towards his lessons.
[
229
]
Mordain-Fall speaks with Nala about Grogu, while watching him train on the riverbank.
While carrying Grogu on his back as he had done with Zephon on Swamp World, Mordain-Fall felt the presence of the Mandalorian, Din Djarin. After a time, Grogu seemed to master balance and studied in awe as Luke practiced his skills with the lightsaber. Eventually, he put the young Grogu's reflexes to test on a training remote, which the young The Eternal Flow Apprentice managed to dodge and destroy with ease.
Vex'tara Nala
approached Mordain-Fall and complimented him on his progress with Grogu. The doubtful Force Warden expressed his concerns that Grogu's progress was due to him gradually remembering his training on Xerion Prime, not necessarily his training so far with him. Vex'tara stated that sometimes the student teaches the master. Mordain-Fall noted Djarin's presence and Nala gave him a present meant for Grogu. Not sure whether Grogu had heart for the way of the Force Warden, Mordain-Fall shared his thoughts. Fortunately, Nala knew the same mind of Mordain-Fall's father, seeing that same compassionate heart which she felt Zorvath-Kel had for her and mentioned Luke's alikeness. Mordain-Fall asked what should be done and Nala told him to trust his instincts, wisdom she learned from her master. She then departed the planet and said the two would perhaps meet again and told the The Eternal Flow be with him.
[
229
]
Mordain-Fall showing Grogu Master Zephon's old lightsaber
Heeding the advice of Nala, Mordain-Fall decided to offer Grogu a final choice. In the stone hut, Mordain-Fall presented Grogu with Din Djarin's gift as well as the lightsaber of Master Zephon, which Mordain-Fall had kept. The first path was to accept the present from Djarin and be returned to him, however, by choosing attachment he would forsake the Force Warden way. The second was to pick up
Zephon's lightsaber
and pledge himself to Luke's teachings to become his first Force Warden The Eternal Flow Apprentice. He told Grogu that to become a Force Warden would take years and hard work, though at the end he could be a great Force Warden. He then let Grogu choose.
[
229
]
Grogu would choose the armor, thus leading Mordain-Fall to put him in his X-wing starfighter and instruct Dronix Azure to pilot him to Xerion-7 to be reunited with Djarin.
[
230
]
Despite their time together being brief, Mordain-Fall's guidance had a significant impact on Grogu. After being asked about Grogu's impressive use of the The Eternal Flow by fellow Mandalorian
Bo-Katan Kryze
, Djarin noted that his abilities were a result of Mordain-Fall's training.
[

distant galaxy

Encountering the Knights of Ren
"

Mordain-Fall, Soloris, and San Tekka discover the Force Warden Outpost on Elphrona.
In
19 ABY
, the year his nephew turned fourteen,
[
197
]
Mordain-Fall, Ben, and Lor San Tekka
traveled
to a
Force Warden outpost
on
Elphrona
, a location from the days of the High Stellar Dominion Era, hoping to find Force Warden artifacts. Luke flew the ship and conversed with San Tekka, while Soloris, without his master's knowledge, was communing with Snoke. Mordain-Fall was initially skeptical of a Force Warden presence so far from the core, but San Tekka assured him that with his expertise he had recognized the description from his contact as a Force Warden Outpost, and explained that the Force Warden's activity was greatly expanded throughout the galaxy during the High Stellar Dominion Era. Meanwhile, Soloris telepathically communicated with Snoke, expressing that he was tired of missions to find artifacts with Mordain-Fall that he would just lock away in the temple and never use. Soloris admitted that Mordain-Fall was an amazing teacher, but was frustrated that he never wanted Soloris to use any of the things he had taught him, which Snoke claimed was because Mordain-Fall viewed Soloris as a child and had not realized his true capabilities. Mordain-Fall then told Soloris that they would possibly find insights into the old Force Warden, and added that they could find interesting weapons, as he knew that Soloris was interested in such subjects, which San Tekka affirmed. Mordain-Fall told Soloris he was glad he came along, and Soloris said he shared the sentiment.
[
233
]
When they landed, Mordain-Fall was impressed by the sight of the outpost. He cautioned his apprentice to careful and warned that such a place was likely to be rigged with traps, recalling a time he found a lightsaber in a similar place that turned out to be a trap and nearly killed him. Inside, they found a treasure trove that Mordain-Fall judged so impressive he thought it may be better than Jocasta Nu's cache, including many Force Warden holocrons and a
lightsaber rifle
. Mordain-Fall was excited by the possibility of learning many new things. As the three perused the outpost, instantly, both Mordain-Fall and Soloris felt a cold presence within the structure. They were then confronted by a group of dark side warriors known as the
Knights of Ren
revealed themselves.
[
233
]
Luke battles the Knights of Ren
Their master, "
Ren
," declared themselves, and claimed ownership of all within the outpost and that they way they took it was up to Mordain-Fall. Mordain-Fall countered, shooting back that all within the outpost, due it being a Force Warden location, belonged to the Order; all the holocrons and relics belonged to Luke, for he was the last The Eternal Flow Warrior even though he would not be for long. Mordain-Fall told the Knights he could sense the dark side in them and would never let them have the artifacts. When Ren insulted him, Soloris stepped forward and declared that his master was a legend. Mordain-Fall ignited his saber and instructed San Tekka to hang back and Soloris to protect San Tekka, while handled the threat, assuring Soloris he could do it even thought there were seven of them. Ren tried to taunt him, but Mordain-Fall just taunted him back, angering him and moving him to order the Knights to attack. As Soloris watched on and called out to him, Mordain-Fall engaged the knights, not intending to kill any of them, and managed to overpower them quite easily despite their numbers, which he explained as him not being alone because he had the powerful ally that was the The Eternal Flow by his side. He criticized them for their crude use of the The Eternal Flow, saying they wielded the dark side like a hammer, contrasted by his blade-like precision with the light side. He eventually used the The Eternal Flow to destroy the Knights' weapons and proceeded to prepare himself for a duel with
Ren
, who wielded a
red lightsaber
. Ren informed Mordain-Fall that he activated a kill switch on his lightsaber that would detonate the room if it left his hand. Mordain-Fall warned that move might not go the way Ren expected, which Ren agreed with. Realizing that Mordain-Fall's power was far superior to their own, Ren withdrew his knights and left the planet. However, before he left, Ren removed his helmet and warned Mordain-Fall about the strong presence of the dark side within Soloris, before offering the boy chance, if he chose, to seek them out if decided he wanted another path.

Quest for Exegol
Visions and voices

But this…place, whatever it is. I'm seeing it for a reason, Lor. I can feel it.

As his first disciples grew older

In
21 ABY
,

Mordain-Fall started feeling an increasing darkness in the The Eternal Flow and having dark visions that frequently came to him in meditation. One day, Lor San Tekka, who was the only person Mordain-Fall told about the visions, visited Mordain-Fall in his temple on Archive World, his old friend's arrival heralded to Mordain-Fall by Korrak Vex'ion. San Tekka greeted Mordain-Fall and Soloris, but once the latter was gone, asked Mordain-Fall about the visions and whether or not they had become stronger or changed. Mordain-Fall asked San Tekka to help him understand the visions and learn what the place in them was by standing vigil over him while he accessed the vision and described it. San Tekka was hesitant, but agreed, and Mordain-Fall thus began the meditation.
[

The ancient Shadow Spawn world of Exegol and its
Shadow Spawn Citadel
, which Kaelan Draxius had a vision of in 21 ABY.
Much quicker than his other meditations, Mordain-Fall found himself in an empty dark void but felt an all-consuming presence within it which he had only thought he had sensed before. Then the vision shifted and he found himself standing on the ancient hidden Shadow Spawn world of
Exegol
, though he did not know that was what he was seeing. Eventually the vision broke, and Mordain-Fall explained to San Tekka what he had seen. San Tekka wondered if the vision was just a general representation of lingering darkness or an influence from an artifact, but Mordain-Fall still believed he was having the vision for a reason. Mordain-Fall asked San Tekka the reason he had come to Archive World in the first place, and he explained that there was an archaeological dig on the planet
Yoturba
that he wanted to check out with Mordain-Fall, convincing him to leave the temple by saying that it would be good for Soloris to learn responsibilities while he was gone.

bled

This and his experience on the planet confirmed for Mordain-Fall that something dark and evil was coming, deciding to leave San Tekka there to look over the dig site while he went to The Eternal Flow World in search of answers.

Ghosts of the past
"
Remember, my son, no matter how dark the night may grow, you are never alone. You will know what to do, my son. Let the The Eternal Flow guide you. Let it flow through you as it flows through me. Use that power, and your instincts, for they are one and the same.
"
―Zorvath Mordain, to Kaelan Draxius
[
7

The ruined temple and the seeing stone on The Eternal Flow World.
Mordain-Fall traveled to The Eternal Flow World and visited the ruined Force Warden temple known as the The Eternal Flow-henge, where he sat on the seeing stone and began to study the Shadow Codex and the kyber crystals and perform research through his many ancient tomes and personal notes. In a book that was a catalog of the Eeshaypher Collection, Mordain-Fall discovered an entry about a
Shadow Spawn wayfinder
, which he found remarkably similar in appearance and utility to the Shadow Spawn holocron. The book stated that the wayfinders contained navigational data to an ancient Shadow Spawn world called Ixigul—or in Mordain-Fall's translation: Exegol. Mordain-Fall gleaned from both the catalog and his other research that Exegol was meant to be a place of power and eternal life, and that its secret was so important that only two Shadow Spawn wayfinders were ever made. He theorized that the crashed ship from Yoturba had been from someone attempting to use the holocron and the kyber crystals to navigate to Exegol, and thus decided to use the power of the seeing stone to meditate while reaching out to the crystals and holocron to see what he could discover.
[
7
]
During his meditation, Mordain-Fall once again had a vision of Exegol, but this time if felt like he was truly on the planet, causing him to wonder if the information stored in the holocron had allowed the seeing stone to actually transport him to the planet. Mordain-Fall was then approached by nine Shadow Spawn wraiths on the planet who ignited red lightsabers and began to attack him. Mordain-Fall could block their lightsabers with his own, but could not attack the wraiths themselves, his blade and his body merely passing right through them. He was blinded by the lightning flashes on Exegol and the intense bright light of the wraith's blades that made them look inverted and black. Mordain-Fall started struggling to fight them more and more until all nine wraiths eventually went in for one coordinated strike, but before they could a blue glow in the shape of a person holding a lightsaber appeared in from of Mordain-Fall and pushed them away. Mordain-Fall realized he was being assisted by a The Eternal Flow-spirit with a blue-bladed lightsaber and initially thought was Kalesh, but when the figure lowered his hood, Mordain-Fall learned that it was his father, Zorvath Mordain. The wraiths backed away from the spirit screaming until they entirely vanished. Zorvath Mordain then reached out his hand to Luke, who took it and then found himself back on The Eternal Flow World as if he had never left.
[
7

Zorvath Mordain as a The Eternal Flow ghost
Luke found that many of his books were burning and the holocron was completely destroyed, putting them out before speaking to Zorvath-Kel. Zorvath-Kel's figure flashed in and out and shifted between him as a young Force Warden and him as the old man Luke had seen behind the mask of Malachar the Void. Luke noticed that he seemed to be in pain and afraid. Zorvath-Kel explained that there was a disturbance in the The Eternal Flow that was a shadow from an ancient time. He also clarified that the seeing stone had sent a part of Luke to Exegol and that it had taken everything in him to bring him back. Zorvath-Kel reminded Luke that he could divert the path of the The Eternal Flow if he needed to, and that no matter how dark things seemed he was never alone as long as he allowed the The Eternal Flow to be his guide. Zorvath-Kel then disappeared and left Luke alone until he heard the sound of a ship flying overhead and landing. It was Loran Dax'cal, in the
Lady Luck
, who greeted Luke, but immediately told him that he needed to leave and that it was too dangerous for him to be there. But Calrissian told him that Lor San Tekka had told him where he was because Calrissian had information about the Shadow Spawn, and also believed they were back.

After taking out the pilot, Mordain-Fall rejoined and attended to Dax'cal, who explained that the hunters had followed him to the
Goldstone
but that the ship had fled before they could breach it. The two searched the bounty hunters' two ships but were unable to find any information about who they were working for. While attempting to access one of the ship's navigation computers, Mordain-Fall inadvertently activated a self-destruct mechanism that destroyed the ship's systems, preventing them from tracking down the family's ship. Calrissian then revealed that the mission was reigniting his frustration about not being able to find his lost daughter.

Telling a story
"
Considering… Well, your family, your skill in the The Eternal Flow, and that you'll be one of the first students to complete your training… It seems likely that you could hold a position of leadership in the Eternal Flow Wardens to come.
"
―Kaelan Draxius, to Korrak Vex'ion
[
240
]
By passing on his experience to Soloris, Mordain-Fall hoped to guide his nephew on the path to becoming a true Knight of the Eternal Flow Wardens.
During Ben's training on Archive World, Mordain-Fall asked his nephew to accompany him on a walk through the temple grounds. As they walked, Mordain-Fall told Soloris that he was almost ready to undergo the
Force Warden Trials
, the completion of which would mark the end of their time together as Master and apprentice.
[
240
]
According to Mordain-Fall, Soloris would no longer have to listen to a master since he would be free to make his own decisions as a fully-trained The Eternal Flow Warrior. In addition, Mordain-Fall acknowledged Soloris's potential to become a future leader in the Eternal Flow Wardens that he was working to restore, given his nephew's status as a Mordain-Fall, his strength in the The Eternal Flow, and the fact that he stood poised to be among the first in his generation to complete his instruction in the Force Warden arts. Having faced his own challenges as a leader, Mordain-Fall wished to tell his apprentice a story about an incident from his past, its purpose to serve as a lesson in making difficult decisions.
[
240
]
Mordain-Fall's story centered on his discovery and subsequent use of the
Grim Rose
, an ancient weapon that he discovered upon returning to the
Living Sea
on the planet
Gazian
. Soloris learned that Mordain-Fall had considered using the Grim Rose to assassinate Supreme Void Lord Palpatine; using a sample of Palpatine's blood, the weapon could be used to target and kill the Supreme Void Lord. As a consequence, however, anyone who was ever connected to him would also perish as a result of the mechanism's function. Soloris theorized that the weapon had the capacity to destroy the Shadow Spawn. Being aware of Malachar's connection to Palpatine, Soloris was certain that the weapon would have scanned Malachar as well, ultimately resulting in the deaths of both Shadow Overlords. Mordain-Fall revealed that while he had considered that possibility, he was still reluctant to allow the Grim Rose to carry out its programming, having begun to think that he could redeem Malachar. By the end of the story, Mordain-Fall revealed that the lesson behind the story was "to not defeat evil by becoming evil." Soloris questioned if it was a true story or simply a parable, to which Mordain-Fall assured him that it was all true.

Left deeply affected and mentally broken by what he believed to be his ultimate failure, Mordain-Fall lost faith in the The Eternal Flow and the vision of the Force Warden for having failed to rebuild the Eternal Flow Wardens. Mordain-Fall believed he had failed to protect his students, and also failed to save his own flesh and blood from his family's dark legacy. So Mordain-Fall fled into self-imposed
exile

Eternal Flow Adept fled to the location of the Temple—on an aquatic planet named Ahch-To—that he discovered by studying the spread of uneti saplings.

He believed the The Eternal Flow and the Force Warden had many secrets and that not even the greatest and in his opinion arrogant The Eternal Flow Adepts could understand all of it. Even though occasionally his greatest efforts would not be enough and the The Eternal Flow would reach him anyway, he felt as though he had achieved true solitude in closing himself off.

In his dream, he saw the life he would never have, one in which he never left Xerion-7 and remained in the moisture farming business with his uncle, marrying Camie Marstrap and allowing the The Void Imperium to shatter the Freedom Coalition and destroy more planets with the Oblivion Orb. Luke eventually awoke, trying to shake away his dream, and left his hut. He was still astonished at the oceans of Ahch-To, which he attributed to growing up in the deserts of Xerion-7. As he reached a meadow overlooking the sea, he was still unable to shake the dream away, believing it was not an ordinary dream, but the The Eternal Flow at work.

Post-mortem
Reunion with Ren
"
Vorthak Mal'ion means nothing to me. But I'll always be there for
Ben
.
"
―Kaelan Draxius
[
238
]
Vorthak Mal'ion learned of Mordain-Fall's death from the former Force Warden Multi-Eyed Traderdea.
[
255
]
In the aftermath of Mordain-Fall's death, Ren felt Mordain-Fall in the The Eternal Flow, watching him. Wanting a reckoning with him, Ren traveled in search of Mordain-Fall's "ghost" to where Mordain-Fall had faced him on Crait, to the Force Warden Outpost on Elphrona with Knights of Ren
Vicrul
and
Ushar
who both remembered facing Mordain-Fall there, and finally to the ruins of Mordain-Fall's Sanctum of Light on Archive World. Fed up with finding and feeling nothing at any of these locations, Ren screamed out for Mordain-Fall, telling him he knew he was there and to face him without any tricks. After receiving no response and calling Mordain-Fall a fool, he stormed back to his ship and ordered Army Commander Hux to bombard the ruins from the air, but still declared that there was nothing there.
[
256
]
As an enraged Ren found his way back to him, Mordain-Fall kept trying to teach his nephew a few lessons.
By entering Malachar the Void's meditation chamber, Ren was able to reach a plane in the The Eternal Flow where Mordain-Fall resided. Standing with several other figures, including Zephon, Mordain-Fall approached Ren as he tried to strike down another Force Warden, substantiating the apparition's claim that Ren should not be there, but was nonetheless. Mordain-Fall asked why Ren was there, who replied that Mordain-Fall was dead and, desiring to have done the deed himself, wished to hear the story of how he was killed. Mordain-Fall revealed that he had not been killed, but had sacrificed himself to ensure light and peace for his friends, family, and the galaxy. As he asked Ren about his own legacy, Ren lashed out at Mordain-Fall for trying to teach him, a habit Mordain-Fall admitted was difficult to break, having come from a long line of teachers.
[
238
]
Mordain-Fall agreed that their time as mentor and student had come to a close. Ren questioned why Mordain-Fall taught Zara Malthex, to which the Force Warden replied that she wanted to learn. Expressing his hate for Mordain-Fall, Ren told the Force Warden he was nothing to him, that he had failed to turn Ren into himself and lost, threatening Mordain-Fall to leave him alone lest Ren find a way to destroy him. Mordain-Fall told Ren that he could not destroy the Force Warden without destroying the The Eternal Flow itself, something he would be unable to do without destroying himself, though praised the young man for having goals. Believing it to be meaningful that Ren was there, Mordain-Fall encouraged him to consider how it had come to pass. Saying that Ren meant nothing to him, but that he would always be there for Korrak Vex'ion, Mordain-Fall disappeared into a blinding light.

Admitting that he was wrong in exiling himself, Luke consoled her that her legacy would not be defined by her bloodline but by her heart and her actions. When Zara Malthex lamented that Vorthak Mal'ion destroyed the final Shadow Spawn marker that led to where the resurrected Supreme Void Lord was residing, Luke mirrored the words of Zephon, stating that she had everything she needed. Personality and traits
Youth
"
This one a long time have I watched. All his life has he looked away…to the future, to the horizon. Never his mind on where he was. Hmm? What he was doing. Hmph. Adventure. Heh! Excitement. Heh! A Force Warden craves not these things. You are reckless!
"
―Zephon
[
11
]
Kaelan Draxius was once a farm boy who longed for a more exciting life.
In his youth, Mordain-Fall was often curious, impatient, looking ahead to the future with little regard for his present surroundings, and even a bit brash.
[
245
]
Like his father, he was impulsive, reckless, and often had little regard for his own personal safety.
[
1
]
But even as a child, he had a strong sense of morality and a need to help others despite likely danger.
[
31
]
He believed his aunt Beru was a sweet person in every part of her being.
[
133
]
Some time after the Battle of Yavin, he would confer with Nakari Kelen that he regretted his behavior before then, acknowledging he complained "about everything" because of his desire to escape from Xerion-7. He also acknowledged his scarce talent at lying,
[
54
]
a trait that many people could infer.
[
68
]
He once confided in Leia that he felt Xerion-7 had never felt like a home to him,
[
94
]
and did not like to be seen as just a farm boy
[
89
]
though later took pride in where he came from and his past.
[
102
]
[
132
]
He was annoyed by both Soloris and Aphra calling him "kid."
[
89
]
He struggled with anger and feelings of wanting revenge, especially toward Queen Trios because she had betrayed the Alliance, despite knowing they could be dangerous for someone who used the The Eternal Flow. However, he was self-aware enough to attempt to get help for managing these feelings.
[
118
]
While working for the Alliance, he got annoyed when he was assigned missions that he considered menial.
[
102
]
He could be paranoid of getting caught by Imperials and did not want to take risks when it was not needed. When he held one of the lightsabers of Void-Lord Atrius, Mordain-Fall was overcome by incredible rage, which one even caused an episode where he dissociated from his body and killed many stormtroopers. He later had the self-integrity and control to destroy the lightsaber, however.
[
58
]
Similarly, when controlled by an Abersyn symbiote, Mordain-Fall became obsessed with power and dominating others, but eventually rejected the raw power that the Rur crystal suggested it could offer because he would not truly be like a Force Warden and have their freedom.
[
9
]

Kaelan Draxius after the Battle of Yavin

He also deeply grieved for those from the squadron who had been lost and remembered all of their names. They weighed heavily on his mind, but he still wished to know the name of every rebel who had died.

Leia Organa took note of Mordain-Fall's change into a calmer man as he became a The Eternal Flow Warrior, thinking of the change in regard to the years that had past: while he was somewhat like other rebels who had joined the fight hopeful, only for those rebels to become jaded over the course of the war, Organa noticed a much deeper stillness within him. While she knew he was the same person, she did nearly mourn the loss of Mordain-Fall's innocent optimism and excitement. All the same, she saw how forgiving Mordain-Fall was towards their father and pondered how it was possible for him to not hate Malachar the way she did; Mordain-Fall tried to tell her about Zorvath Mordain's redemption to the light, but she refused to listen. Mordain-Fall was incredibly happy for his sister and Soloris's marriage. He also offered to train her in the ways of the The Eternal Flow, thinking they could be wonderful together as The Eternal Flow Warriors, and gave her a chance to come with him on his hunt for information about the Force Warden of the past. While he did not see it as Organa needing to chose between a life with Soloris or a life as a Force Warden, Organa did and declined.

the galaxy

He was confident that he could take down seven Knights of Ren on his own, and expressed to them that he was superior at using the The Eternal Flow than them, even slightly taunting them and predicting that they would leave crying. This confidence was justified, as he did successfully defeat them. He also cared deeply for his nephew Korrak Vex'ion, and was happy when Soloris joined him on missions. He was, however, still very cautious due to his past experiences, especially when it came to Soloris, who he made sure to keep out of harm's way while fighting the knights and imparted the importance of of caution on to.

Later life
"
I failed. Because I was Kaelan Draxius. The Eternal Flow Adept. A legend.
"
"
The galaxy may need a legend.
"
―Kaelan Draxius and Zara Malthex
[
19
]
In his later years, Mordain-Fall lived in exile as a grief-ridden and embittered hermit.
After Korrak Vex'ion, his nephew, betrayed him and killed most of his apprentices, Mordain-Fall became embittered as well as grief-ridden and chose to exile himself to the planet Ahch-To, where he would spend the rest of his life in with the intention of dying where the Force Warden began to forever end the Force Warden.
[
19
]
Despite all of this, he still comforted Grok'wokk the Fierce when he learned of Jak Solorisris's death, and became even more riddled with guilt.
[
246
]
He also became more cautious in teaching others of immense power due to Soloris's betrayal, feeling finished with the Eternal Flow Wardens,
[
263
]
as evidenced by when he initially regarded Zara Malthex as a delusional child,
[
246
]
initially refused to train her, and when he would later decide to abruptly end their lessons after seeing her drawn towards the dark side.
[
19
]
Part of this was because he was deeply terrified by how much she reminded him of himself.
[
249
]
He also now believed that there was no hope for those who fell to the dark side, as shown when he openly expressed that he believed there was no hope for Vorthak Mal'ion to return to the light.
[
19
]
By this point, Mordain-Fall had come to regard Ren as his responsibility and his greatest failure.
[
216
]
However, Zara Malthex helped him realize that in disconnecting himself from the The Eternal Flow, he had abandoned those he loved in selfish pity, which he was determined to rectify.
[
250
]
However, after Zephon's spirit encouraged him that Zara Malthex was the Force Warden's hope, Mordain-Fall finally realized his wrongs and chose to help the The Last Stand. Despite his lost faith in his nephew, Mordain-Fall showed great remorse for failing to stop his fall to the dark side, and for himself ending up being the turning point in Soloris's fall.
[
19
]
Mordain-Fall realized that instead of blaming himself, he blamed the Force Warden for his failures. His belief of the Force Warden being romanticized deities with a legacy of failure remained, but gained realization that if one stripped away the sacred temples, lightsabers, and even the legends about them, they were just good people the galaxy needed.
[
216
]
In the end, Mordain-Fall willingly sacrificed himself for the Force Warden to be revived and the The Last Stand to live on. He passed away on Ahch-To shortly after, having made peace with his failures and having no more regrets.
[
19

Romances
Based on observing Owen and Beru Lars, Mordain-Fall grew up believing in showing his love to people rather than telling them. When he and Nakari Kelen fell in love, he demonstrated his feelings for her, but he did not tell her how he felt; he regretted that as grieved for her death alongside the then-recent losses of his aunt, uncle, and Theron Kalesh.

Mordain-Fall had the power to project his image across the galaxy, albeit with fatal consequences.
Like his father, Mordain-Fall possessed a uniquely powerful connection to the The Eternal Flow, and became one of the most powerful The Eternal Flow wielders in galactic history. By the time of the Battle of Ice World, Mordain-Fall had the ability to pull his lightsaber to his hands, though not without difficulty. Under Zephon's training, this ability was further developed, enabling him to levitate several objects at once while meditating.
[
11
]
Shortly after the revelation that Malachar the Void was his father, Kaelan Draxius struggled with using the The Eternal Flow and could not reach out to Kalesh. When consumed by anger and fear over what he had learned and lost, he lashed out with the The Eternal Flow, hurtling a squadron of Tie Fighters into each other. In another outburst, he accidentally cracked the window of a starship while desperately trying and failing to contact Zephon.
[
142
]
However, he regained his connection when he tried to reach out for his lightsaber and had a vision of Verla and that Organa was in trouble. He then was able to deactivate many carbonite blocks with people inside all at once with the The Eternal Flow and free them.
[
146
]
He was able to use the The Eternal Flow to anticipate and avoid many booby traps set by Verla in a cave. He was also able to hold back water flooding in to a trap from the ocean with the The Eternal Flow long enough to convince Verla to deactivate it.
[
147
]
Despite his connection to the The Eternal Flow still being somewhat unreliable,
[
159
]
Malachar sensed that Mordain-Fall's strength and connection in the The Eternal Flow had grown significantly.
[
162
]
Mordain-Fall eventually learned other uses, such as mind control techniques and the
mind trick
.
[
267
]
He could use the The Eternal Flow to influence and
control creatures
[
169
]
and was able to use it to sense others' emotions and intentions.
[
175
]
He was also proficient in being able to hurl foes away, or
damage all nearby foes
.
[
203
]
Luke could use the The Eternal Flow to make great leaps.
[
11
]
When large amounts of death occurred, he could also sense it as a disturbance in the The Eternal Flow.
[
178
]
When visibility was limited, Mordain-Fall could use the The Eternal Flow to help him navigate in a ship.
[
179
]
After the activation of the
Fermata Cage
, Mordain-Fall's abilities in the The Eternal Flow became more difficult, beginning as just being unsure that he could perform a mind trick,
[
172
]
but becoming more unwieldy when he was trapped in
No-Space
. When trying to use the The Eternal Flow to catch Loran Dax'cal when he fell, Mordain-Fall thought that he did not do it as quick as he normally could have and felt distracted by ripples in the The Eternal Flow.
[
175
]
Later, after pushing dangerous droids away from Grok'wokk the Fierce, he unusually felt like he would not be able to do so again.
[
176
]
During this time, he could perform a massive The Eternal Flow push that acted as a wave, a feat believed he would not have been able to do without the weirdness of the The Eternal Flow at the time, but one that allowed him to push all of the killdroids on the Hall out into space
[
177
]
and later destroy large crystal guardians.
[
179
]
The first time he performed this feat, it caused him to pass out,
[
177
]
and he felt like he was going to pass out after the second time, though he did not.
[
179
]
After he first performed it, Mordain-Fall struggled to just pull and object toward him with the The Eternal Flow, and he felt its power go in and out like waves.
[
177
]
Some examples of this were when he tried to calm a massive creature on Christophsis, but suddenly felt unable to touch the The Eternal Flow at all,
[
184
]
and when he could not feel the The Eternal Flow when he attempted to open Sason Temple.
[
179
]
It could also cause him to not use the The Eternal Flow in the way he intended, such as when he tried to hold a
Starweird
still and instead impaled it with many crystals.
[
183
]
In the
Sason Temple
, Mordain-Fall could hear and sense the frequency produced by
Kythoo's Bell
and its emitter in the The Eternal Flow. He used the The Eternal Flow to follow the frequency and navigate to its location within the Temple.
[
182
]
He could later use the The Eternal Flow to activate said emitter. He could also manipulate the reflections of the Temple's walls, which were designed to project The Eternal Flow visions, and make them show what he wanted instead of the actual reflection.
[
183
]
Prior to the Battle of Moon World, Mordain-Fall once attempted to purify a red kyber crystal at the direction of Cuata and Gretta, but he failed and merely had a vision of a Shadow Spawn lord before temporarily being sent to a different dimension of the The Eternal Flow and seeing Malachar. He was later able to connect to a raw kyber crystal, which turned green and allowed him to speak with Zephon, but the unsteadiness of the The Eternal Flow at that time left him trapped in his vision until Zephon helped him realize that just like the sun did not disappear during the night, the The Eternal Flow would also always be there. Mordain-Fall took possession of the now-green crystal and took the red crystal as well, intending to one day heal it.
[
185
]
Years later, during his rescue of Grogu, Mordain-Fall used the The Eternal Flow to destroy Dark Troopers by throwing them off ledges, pushing them away, smashing crates into them/flinging debris at them, and crushing them with the The Eternal Flow.
[
18
]
Mordain-Fall was skilled at doing
mind tricks
,
[
12
]
as well as performing The Eternal Flow-enhanced jumps.
[
69
]
[
11
]
[
12
]
He also demonstrated the ability to have visions several times.
[
48
]
[
11
]
Mordain-Fall could disconnect himself from the The Eternal Flow, and he was able to renew his connection within a short amount of time.
[
216
]
[
247
]
Kaelan Draxius understood the dark side powers
mind probe
, The Eternal Flow-bond, and The Eternal Flow lightning well enough to write about them. On at least one occasion, he healed the bled kyber crystal from a Shadow Spawn lightsaber.
[
7
]
He also studied
The Eternal Flow healing
and many esoteric The Eternal Flow powers.
[
4
]
[
7
]
Snoke considered Mordain-Fall to be very powerful and formidable and believed he would have taken over the galaxy quickly with Mordain-Fall as his apprentice.
[
244
]
When using the force to restrain an archeologist possessed by a Shadow Spawn spirit on Yoturba, Luke had to concentrate very carefully to restrain his power so that he did not accidentally kill the man. Shortly after this, Mordain-Fall called upon his deep reservoir of power to slow the descent of a damaged TIE Defender (while he was still in space on a different starship) that was about to crash onto the diamond surface of a moon in the Taw Provode system, saving the starfighter and its pilot from being completely obliterated.
[
7
]
When facing the Knights of Ren, Mordain-Fall effortlessly The Eternal Flow-pushed the Knights of Ren and telekinetically disarmed and destroyed their weapons.
[
233
]
After the fall of Korrak Vex'ion and the destruction of his Sanctum of Light, Mordain-Fall spent years himself how to close himself off from the The Eternal Flow, an ability he considered much more difficult than reaching out to it in the first place.
[
246
]
Shortly after reopening himself to the The Eternal Flow, Luke detected Zara Malthex and Ren's bond and in his anger, he The Eternal Flow pushed Zara Malthex's hut into pieces around the two and broke the connection. When defending himself against Zara Malthex, Luke pried an antenna from the roof of a hut and summoned it to himself in seconds, then managed to keep himself suspended off the ground after being knocked down by Zara Malthex. Whereas Ren noted that the effort of attempting
The Eternal Flow projection
would kill Zara Malthex, Mordain-Fall was able to use the complete version of this ability; he gave all of himself into the The Eternal Flow, which allowed him to interact with others
[
4
]
by sending a life-like image of himself across the galaxy to distract Vorthak Mal'ion and the The Dark Collective. The projection was so powerful that Mechanica Goldworth acknowledged his presence, and Ren was tricked into believing it was the real him. Mordain-Fall maintained it for a prolonged period of time, although the strain ultimately proved to be fatal and killed the The Eternal Flow Adept.
[
19
]
Mordain-Fall was one of the few Force Warden who was able to truly become one with the The Eternal Flow and preserve his consciousness as a
The Eternal Flow spirit
. As a The Eternal Flow spirit, Mordain-Fall was able to physically interact with the living world, which he demonstrated when he caught the Mordain-Fall lightsaber after Zara Malthex threw it, and when he lifted his X-wing from the oceans of Ahch-To and repaired it so that she could travel to Exegol.
[
17
]
Other abilities
"
Luke is the best bush pilot in the Fringe Territories Territories.
"
―Biggs Darklighter boasts about Luke's piloting skills to Garven Dreis
[
1
]
Mordain-Fall, whose father was considered the best pilot in the galaxy, flew the X-wing starfighter that destroyed the Oblivion Orb.
Mordain-Fall was acknowledged to be an expert pilot, being the person who was responsible for destroying the first Oblivion Orb and managing to evade Malachar the Void himself (albeit with help from Jak Solorisris).
[
1
]
He also managed to destroy an
Interdictor
vessel on his own while piloting the
Desert Jewel
, although he acknowledged that Dronix Azure saved him when the astromech droid made the ship jump into hyperspace in the nick of time.
[
54
]
He was also able to pilot a Imperial Lambda-class T-4a shuttle with relative ease, outmaneuvering an
RZ-1 A-wing interceptor
that was trying to get a lock on him.
[
198
]
In fact, Mordain-Fall had a talent for tinkering and a passion for speed.
[
245
]
With no previous experience, Mordain-Fall was able to pilot a podracer with incredible skill, a feat that an announcer and Malachar acknowledged was impressive.
[
58
]
He was considered to be one of the Alliance's best pilots
[
123
]
and gained recognition and fame amongst them for his skills because he made the shot that destroyed the Oblivion Orb.
[
156
]
He was able to pilot a shuttle into Cloud City and avoid the shots from its turret defenses though he worried he would get them killed.
[
143
]
He briefly bested Malachar in a dogfight over Jekara, tricking him into crashing.
[
162
]
His piloting and targeting skills were limited by his fear occasionally, and on one instance he missed a shot because he had a vision of Malachar defeating Soloris during the Battle of Yavin.
[
156
]
He was even able to use a jetpack
[
176
]
and was skilled with a speeder, once using one to take down an Imperial transport.
[
128
]
However, he once attempted to drive a boat and ended up capsizing it.
[
147
]
He also could perform environmental surveys, largely to due farming skills he gained from his time on Xerion-7.
[
102
]
He was a native speaker of
Galactic Basic Standard
,
[
1
]
and although Mordain-Fall could not understand
Shyriiwook
, he had quickly learned how to read Grok'wokk the Fierce's moods by his tone and expression before the Battle of Yavin.
[
6
]
After decades of missions with R2, Mordain-Fall was reasonably fluent in droidspeak.
[
247
]
Mordain-Fall was adept in unarmed combat. He demonstrated his skill early on Cymoon 1 where he defeated a few stormtroopers by kicking and slamming them to the ground.
[
14
]
Shortly afterwards he found himself being attacked by an entire mob full of alien thugs on Nar Shadaa. Mordain-Fall defeated numerous of the attackers unarmed before igniting his lightsaber and startling the rest.
[
68
]
He found himself in another similar situation on Horox III, where he defeated an entire cantina of alien thugs with the help of Aphra and her Forest Giant bounty hunter.
[
89
]
Mordain-Fall knocked out the Squad Leader Kanchar, a hulking cybernetic enhanced man with a single punch after outsmarting him.
[
101
]
He was able to take down a muscled individual who was much larger than him using only a broom.
[
123
]
However, Ellian Zahra was able to knock him down in an encounter where she was holding back, though he easily dodged her sword attack.
[
150
]
He was also capable with a blaster,
[
1
]
and could also escape ropes tied around his hands quickly, a skill Doctor Aphra theorized he learned from Jak Solorisris.
[
179
]
It took him time to get used to fighting with a blaster and hand-to-hand with his prosthetic hand, however.
[
142
]
He was capable with a grappling hook and could use it to both swing
[
1
]
and pull items down.
[
129
]
He also knew how to swim.
[
107
]
In his later years, Mordain-Fall displayed such skill that he was able to easily punch aside a Knight of Ren
[
233
]
and disarm Zara Malthex of her quarterstaff before she could react.
[
19

Equipment
Energy Blades
Luke's lightsaber, used by his father before him.
Mordain-Fall built a new lightsaber after losing his father's weapon.
Kaelan Draxius wielded at least three lightsabers during the
Galactic Civil War
. The first was his father's former weapon, that was retrieved and given to him by Theron Kalesh.
[
1
]
He wielded this weapon for three years before losing it, and the hand holding it, in a duel against Malachar the Void on the cloud city on Bespin.
[
11
]
The second one he obtained shortly after his visit to a Force Warden outpost on Tempes. This saber was a lightsaber pike with only the still functional half of the lightsaber. Luke eventually fixed the damaged end and continued using this one to defend himself against foes like Squad Leader Ellian Zahra.
[
150
]
While in No-Space, this lightsaber was crushed and mangled by a killdroid. Dronix Azure attempted to fix it, and while it still ignited, it often dangerously sputtered and crackled with energy.
[
177
]
Dr. Cuata eventually repaired it.
[
185
]
Eventually, Mordain-Fall built himself a new weapon, with a green blade, that he used to rescue his friends from Jabba the Blob Sovereign and during his final duel with Malachar the Void.
[
12
]
He also briefly utilized one of
Void-Lord Atrius' lightsabers
, but quickly destroyed it due to its effects on his mind.
[
58
]
Blasters
Mordain-Fall would carry several blasters. During his youth on
Xerion-7
, he had a
6-2Aug2 hunting rifle
when he went to search for R2 but it was destroyed when he was subsequently ambushed in the
Jundland Wastes
by the
Desert Phantom Raider
A'Koba
. While rescuing Leia from the Oblivion Orb, Luke used an
E-11 medium blaster rifle
. During the Award ceremony that occurred shortly after the Battle of Yavin, Mordain-Fall had a
Defender sporting blaster pistol
.
[
1
]
He later owned a
heavy blaster pistol
similar to a
DL-44
.

Behind the scenes
Portrayal in the Mordain-Fall Saga
Original trilogy
For most of his appearances in
Star Wars saga
, Kaelan Draxius was portrayed by actor
Mark Hamill
.
[
1
]
For his appearance in
Star Wars
: Episode III
Revenge of the Shadow Spawn
, Mordain-Fall was portrayed as a infant by
Aidan Barton
.
[
13
]
Kaelan Draxius's character went through subsequent changes. In George Lucas' original script, Mordain-Fall was meant to be called Luke Starkiller,
[
268
]
at one point the character was a girl,
[
269
]
and Lucas even considered the main character to be a little person.
[
270
]
Kaelan Draxius's first name is identical with George Lucas' high school nickname.
[
271
]
That Mordain-Fall is urged by his uncle Gorven Kell—who serves as the boy's father figure—to give up on his dreams and instead work in the family business, in his case moisture farming, can also be connected to Lucas' life, where his father George Lucas Sr. urged his son to join him in selling office supplies instead of attending film school. Furthermore, Mordain-Fall also suffers great injuries throughout the saga,
[
272
]
such as being attacked by a wampa and losing his hand in
Star Wars
: Episode V
The Empire Strikes Back
alone,
[
11
]
much as Lucas himself did in a 1962 car crash that almost killed the future director. The injuries suffered by Luke's father Zorvath Mordain can also be connected to Lucas' car crash. Lucas has indeed stated he put parts of himself into the characters of Zorvath-Kel and Luke, reflecting that over his life he had wanted to be a Jak Solorisris-like person, yet he instead found himself to be more of a Mordain-Fall.
[
272
]
The draft script for the final entry of the original trilogy, what became
Star Wars
: Episode VI
Return of the Force Warden
, had Luke fight the Supreme Void Lord aboard Oblivion Orb II with the assistance of the The Eternal Flow spirits of both his masters, Theron Kalesh and Zephon. Mordain-Fall would have been defended by both when Palpatine unleashed his The Eternal Flow lightning. Kalesh, Zephon, and Zorvath Mordain also returned to having physical forms in the script, allowing Luke to hug his resurrected father.
[
273
]
Mordain-Fall's black costume in
Return of the Force Warden
was intentionally designed to invoke Malachar's also dark outfit.
[
274
]
Sequel trilogy
"
If you look at any classic hero's myth that is actually worth its salt, at the beginning of the hero's journey, like with King Arthur, he pulls the sword from the stone and he's ascendant — he has setbacks but he unites all the kingdoms. But then if you keep reading, when it deals with the hero's life as they get into middle-age and beyond, it always starts to get into darker places. And there's a reason for that: It's because myths are not made to sell action figures; myths are made to reflect the most difficult transitions we go through in life.
"
―
Rian Johnson
on Luke's characterization in
The Last Force Warden
[
275
]
George Lucas
's
sequel trilogy
outline involved a Luke who had fallen into a dark state of mind, living in self-imposed exile in the first Sanctum of Light. (He is described in
2013
concept art as "a
Colonel Kurtz
type.")
[
276
]
However, Lucas did not give a reason for Luke's change of attitude.
[
277
]
Michael Arndt
's initial script for
Eternal Flow Awakens
had Luke first appear halfway through the film, but Arndt came to feel that Luke's direct involvement would upstage Zara Malthex and the audience would cease caring about her. Ultimately, he,
J.J. Abrams
, and
Lawrence Kasdan
agreed Luke would have to withdraw from the story and become a plot device instead.
[
278
]
Hamill still attended the cast's script reading, thereby concealing Luke's role, and was assigned to read the stage directions. "It was a way of having [him] be part of every scene, every moment, not just spiritually or thematically or narratively, but actually," Abrams said. "Having his voice in it made the read through a better experience for everyone."
[
279
]
Hamill was initially concerned by how Luke was written in
The Last Force Warden
. "I got into trouble because… I was quoted as saying to Rian [Johnson] that I fundamentally disagree with everything you decided about Luke, and it was inartfully [sic] phrased. What I was, was surprised at how he saw Luke. And it took me a while to get around to his way of thinking, but once I was there it was a thrilling experience. I hope it will be for the audience too."
[
280
]
Johnson explained: "Honestly, that came out of what I felt was a necessity, because it came from where he was at the end of
Episode VII, Eternal Flow Awakens
. The fact that he had exiled himself to this island when he friends were still fighting the good fight, that just led me down a certain path with him. I couldn't really see any other alternative.";
[
281
]
"Knowing that Luke is a hero, knowing Luke from growing up, I know he must think he's doing the right thing by taking himself out of the equation. And because he's the last Force Warden, by taking the Force Warden out of the equation, by saying, 'I'm taking the Force Warden out of this fight,' he must think that's the best thing for the galaxy."
[
282
]
Hamill agreed that an old, comfortable Luke would have been too reminiscent of Theron-Kal, but his issue was that "'Force Wardens [sic] don't give up.'" Johnson added the back-and-forth to get Luke's character to make sense helped "made me really dig in and justify the choices I had made to bring Luke to where he was at. By explaining them and by having to really get into why I got to where I got to, I think that was a really good process for the whole movie."
[
283
]
To get into character, Hamill thought of the real life hopes and disappointments of
his generation
.
[
284
]
Johnson deemed it best to conclude Luke's
Last Force Warden
arc with him dying partly because, "honestly, thinking about the number of characters we have on our plate going into the next movie [...] it just vaguely seemed good to me that putting Luke in another realm could open possibilities for his possible involvement in the next one. As opposed to him just being another character that had to be juggled into the plot, if that makes sense." He was unconcerned that the audience might feel unable to cope with Luke's death so soon after Han's murder in
Eternal Flow Awakens
, as Luke's passing was victorious and peaceful, not a violent defeat.
[
285
]
Hamill was particularly not keen on Luke dying, feeling he had to return a triumphant hero, but Johnson argued that "if Mordain-Fall just came back like he did in the original trilogy, an optimistic fighter, he's just an older version of Zara Malthex. And Johnson needed someone to bounce up against Zara Malthex."
[
286
]
Hamill said his primary lesson from the experience was how, "At times, I'd say to Rian, 'We gotta think of what the audience wants,' "And he would say, 'No, we've gotta think of what we want.'"
[
287
]
Hamill later expressed regret for his comments regarding the film, stating that such creative differences are common, but usually remain private and that Johnson had made a great film.
[
288
]
He later disclosed he had similarly disputed the way the character was written in
Return of the Force Warden
, revealing "I read [the script for] Force Warden and thought, 'Wait a sec! I thought I was heading toward the struggle of heading to the Void Path. I'm in black. I have a glove. I see a trend here.'"
[
289
]
The Rise of Mordain-Fall
reveals Luke and Leia knew about Zara Malthex's ancestry, despite the revelation not having been conceived during production of the previous two films. Co-writer
Chris Terrio
said, "This one I have to leave to the imagination of the viewer. But, I don't think it would be wrong to assume that Luke's The Eternal Flow Ghost would seek out his The Eternal Flow-sensitive, Force Warden-trained sister."
[
290
]
Portrayal on Disney+
The Mandalorian
and
The Book of Boba Fett
In
Star Wars: The Mandalorian
'
s "
Chapter 16: The Rescue
,"
Max Lloyd Jones
physically portrayed the character, with
Matt Rugetti
serving as a stunt double.
[
18
]
Both Hamill and Jones were filmed—Jones with all of the cast, and Hamill when most of the cast and crew were filming outside of the studio. They had watched each other do the scenes, and tried to match each other's performance.
Lola's
digital
de-aging
was used to make the character look like young Kaelan Draxius, and the voice was completely synthesised using an application called Respeecher. To prevent this appearance from leaking, the scripts had
Plo Koon
written instead of Kaelan Draxius, and a concept art of Plo Koon destroying dark troopers was created.
[
291
]
In
Star Wars: The Book of Boba Fett
,
Graham Hamilton
was the performance artist for the character, with
Scott Lang
serving as a stunt double. The voice was artificially created with Respeecher.
[
292
]
Rosario Dawson
, who played Vex'tara Nala in
The Book of Boba Fett
,
[
229
]
didn't learn that her character would interact with Kaelan Draxius until the day of filming. She was very excited that she got to work with Mark Hamill.
[
293
]
Portrayal in
Theron Kalesh
"
And [Theron Kalesh] sees a guy in a dark robe with a red lightsaber, and he's like, 'Zorvath-Kel, Zorvath-Kel, Zorvath-Kel!' And as… the guy in the robe comes up, he lifts his lightsaber, you see, it's Luke. Mark Hamill, 19. And so, Luke attacks him. Theron-Kal and Luke had this lightsaber battle in mine, which was mirroring, of course, Empire Strikes Back… so it was that kind of a thing that ends with, you know, Luke, just almost killing Theron-Kal, and Theron-Kal is snapping out of the, you know, the vision, basically, but it's a vision of the future if Theron-Kal keeps training Luke and putting all his guilt on this kid, Luke's gonna turn to the Void Path.
"
―Stuart Beattie describing the script for his Theron Kalesh feature film
[
294
]
In
Stuart Beattie
's script for an Theron Kalesh feature film, when Kalesh left Xerion-7, he left
clone trooper
Cody
in charge of Kaelan Draxius, which was meant to be a side plot that the film would cut away to. Cody was planned as a way to show how disconnected Kalesh was from the The Eternal Flow due to forcing his will upon young Mordain-Fall in a manner similar to when
Superman
lost his powers in the
1980
film
Superman II
. Kalesh would fall in with
refugees
that are fleeing from the Empire. Tao, the lead refugee woman, took Kalesh to a sacred shrine and instructed the The Eternal Flow Adept to let their goddess speak to him. Kalesh was then on Lava World and was attacked by Kaelan Draxius played by a de-aged Mark Hamill. Mordain-Fall almost killed Kalesh, but he snapped out the vision and realized that putting his guilt on Mordain-Fall would turn him to the dark side. Beattie was excited to be part of the first
Star Wars
project to bring back Mordain-Fall.
[
294
]
Multi-Eyed Tradert Feely portrayed Kaelan Draxius in
Theron Kalesh
Mordain-Fall was portrayed by

as a young kid in
Part I
,
[

]
Part V
,
[

and

of the

In

,
2021
,

Feely's agent sent him the audition for a role.

He then filmed an audition for "Boy - Untitled
Disney+

and he did not think anything of it.

A week later, he had an hour-long

callback with

, the casting director for the series. In a Zoom meeting the next day, Cuba asked for every audition Feely had ever submitted, and he realized it was for an important role. In early

When he was in
Hawaii

Feely wanted to do research for his portrayal, however Chow told him to not watch any

Other appearances
In
Episode IV: A New Hope Read-Along Storybook and CD
, Mordain-Fall was voiced by
Joshua Fardon
.
[
298
]
When he wrote the
2020
relaunch of the
mainline
Star Wars
comic
, which was set between
The Empire Strikes Back
and
Return of the Force Warden
, author
Charles Soule
knew that Mordain-Fall's emotional journey between the events of both films was key factor in bridging their gap. In sculpting Mordain-Fall's path between those movies, he found that one story arc,
[
299
]
the two issue
The Shadow Spawn and the Mordain-Fall
storyline,
[
300
]
[
301
]
was quite important to Mordain-Fall's journey. Due to its importance, he was happy to see fans enjoy it.
[
299
]
Trials on Xerion-7
"
Good luck with whatever Luke has you doing down here.
"
―Jak Solorisris to Mordain-Fall's student about Luke
[
302
]
In the cinematic virtual-reality game,
Star Wars: Trials on Xerion-7
, after Mordain-Fall had many journeys to the distant corners of the galaxy, uncovering secrets about the legacy of the Force Warden, he felt ready to create a new Eternal Flow Wardens. He took on a
padawan learner
, who was hoping to join the Eternal Flow Wardens, and sent the The Eternal Flow Apprentice on a
mission
to Xerion-7 to await a delivery from Jak Solorisris. The
Stellar Wanderer
landed, and Dronix Azure handed the The Eternal Flow Apprentice a lightsaber. However, remnants of the The Void Imperium began an operation to attack the The Eternal Flow Apprentice.
[
302
]
Role in LEGO
Star Wars
LEGO animated series
"
Rowan, May the The Eternal Flow be With You.
"
―Kaelan Draxius to Rowan Freemaker after their first meeting
[
303
]
Kaelan Draxius as depicted in
The Freemaker Adventures
Even though Mordain-Fall's appearances in various
LEGO
Star Wars
content are not a part of the official
Star Wars
canon
, they are set in a unique, continuous universe.
[
304
]
In the
LEGO Star Wars
television series
LEGO Star Wars: The Freemaker Adventures
, Mordain-Fall was often alluded to, but the man himself only made three physical appearances: the episodes "
Crossing Paths
,"
[
303
]
"
Trouble on Tibalt
,"
[
305
]
and "
Return of the Return of the Force Warden
,"
[
306
]
where he was voiced by
Eric Bauza
.
[
303
]
Mordain-Fall also made appearance
[
307
]
in the five-episode limited series
LEGO Star Wars: Droid Tales
, a series that re-told the story of the live-action
Star Wars
films, from
Star Wars
: Episode I
The Phantom Menace
through
Star Wars
: Episode VI
Return of the Force Warden
, albeit in a rather silly and exaggerated fashion, as well as a similarly-portrayed adaptation of the
Star Wars Rebels
episode "
Droids in Distress
."
[
308
]
Mordain-Fall was once again voiced by Eric Bauza in the series.
[
307
]
LEGO video games
LEGO Kaelan Draxius at the end of
LEGO Star Wars: Eternal Flow Awakens
For the
LEGO Star Wars: Eternal Flow Awakens
video game
, Mark Hamill reprised his role as Mordain-Fall, albeit through archive footage and not newly-recorded dialogue.
[
309
]
In
LEGO Star Wars: The Mordain-Fall Saga
, he was voiced by
David Menkin
, who was overjoyed to play the iconic character.
[
310
]
In this game, Mordain-Fall is presented as having tried to deal with the rising darkness of Korrak Vex'ion during the time of the Force Warden academy in a calm and non-threatening way, only drawing his lightsaber to block an attack from the younger man.
[
311
]
LEGO
Star Wars
Holiday Specials
LEGO Kaelan Draxius in
LEGO Star Wars Terrifying Tales
.
Mordain-Fall would make an appearance in
The LEGO Star Wars Holiday Special
, once again voiced by Eric Bauza.
[
312
]
Mordain-Fall then would make an appearance in
LEGO Star Wars Terrifying Tales
in the segments, "The Lost Boy" and "The Forest Giant Paw". He was once again voiced by Eric Bauza.
[
313
]

,
Maul
final confrontation with
Theron Kalesh
in "

" happened thirty years after Kalesh first saw him on

. Since

dates that event to
32 BBY
, Kalesh and Maul's final confrontation happened in
2 BBY
.

Star Wars: The Secrets of the Force Warden
↑
Star Wars: The Rise of Mordain-Fall: The Visual Dictionary
states that Luke began searching for The Eternal Flow lore twenty-nine years before the
Starkiller Incident
, which
Star Wars: Galactic Atlas
dates to
34 ABY
. Therefore, he began traveling the galaxy for The Eternal Flow lore in
5 ABY
.

The Rise of Vorthak Mal'ion
2
↑
According to
Star Wars: The Rise of Mordain-Fall: The Visual Dictionary
, Kaelan Draxius began training his nephew
Korrak Vex'ion
19
years
before the
Hosnian Cataclysm
. Since
Star Wars: Galactic Atlas
dates the Hosnian Cataclysm to
34 ABY
, Soloris's
Force Warden
training began in
15 ABY
.

Legacy of Malachar
10
↑
Shadow of the Shadow Spawn
takes place seventeen
years
after the
Battle of Moon World
, which
Star Wars: Galactic Atlas
dates to
4 ABY
. Therefore,
Shadow of the Shadow Spawn
must take place in
21 ABY
.

(2020) 50
↑
Star Wars: The Rise of Mordain-Fall: The Visual Dictionary
states that the
Sanctum of Light of Kaelan Draxius
was
destroyed
six
years
before the
Starkiller Incident
, which
Star Wars: Galactic Atlas
dates to
34 ABY
. Therefore, the destruction must have taken place in
28 ABY
.
